 **⬛ PARTIALLY CLOSED 2026-08-19 (qa-sweep).** **FCRA is retrievable**: 15 U.S.C. §1681m(a)(1)–(4) was retrieved verbatim from law.cornell.edu (200, 131,616 B) and corroborated by the govinfo PDF path, and was used to rule WF5-F2 **fixed** clause by clause. **ESIGN remains unretrieved** (not attempted this session — outside Domain 9's scope). Re-scope this escalation to ESIGN only. **⬛ NARROWED 2026-08-19 (qa-sweep, compliance-auditor).** eCFR, consumerfinance.gov, law.cornell.edu and govinfo all answered **200 with real rule text** this session (bodies grepped, not trusted on status code), so the reachability premise is dead. The two halves now separate: **(a) the notice-wording half is RULED and is a defect** — it is governed by **Reg B §1002.13(c)**, not Reg C, and that text is retrievable and dispositive: *"The creditor shall also inform the applicant(s) that if the applicant(s) chooses not to provide the information, the creditor is required to note the ethnicity, race and sex on the basis of visual observation or surname."* The proposed "it only attaches in person" refutation **fails** — (c)'s disclosure sentence carries no in-person condition; the conditionality lives in (b)'s underlying duty. See ux-11. **(b) the disaggregated-subcategory half REMAINS OPEN** — §1002.13(a)(1)(i) offers an express either/or, and electing (A) the aggregate categories **is compliant**; Reg C would compel Appendix B but **does not yet bind** — §1003.2(g)(2) makes a nondepository covered only after **25 closed-end originations in each of two preceding calendar years**. That is a launch-checklist trigger, not a fix-wave item.# Feature Review — Findings Register

The single register for all verified findings from the feature-review program. Rules and
lifecycle: `CHARTER.md`. Only findings that survived `finding-verifier` (and, where flagged,
`compliance-auditor`) may be added. REFUTED findings are recorded too, so they are not
re-discovered on re-runs.

**Id convention**: audit ids are canonical — `F-###` (product/code), `D-###` (documentation),
`ux-##` (UI/UX), `N-###` (corrections), `U-#` (escalations), `J-<MMDD>-<NN>` (client-journey
seams — `JOURNEYS.md`, minted from the walker's run date, never a next-free integer). Domain
column maps to `DOMAINS.md`; a `J-` row names the two surfaces the seam spans.

> **Seeded 2026-07-08 from a 9-dimension read-only audit** (existence, wiring, tests,
> gating/data-model, workflows, security, compliance-math, documentation ×2, static UI/UX). These
> are *pre-verified* entries; a domain re-run confirms/closes them. Baseline: security posture
> **strong** (no P0/IDOR); numeric compliance tables **verified correct** (N-003).

| J-0820-01 | 2 ↔ 6 (`/e-consent` ↔ `/loan-estimate/:id`) | defect | **P1** | flagged (ESIGN/TRID — consent records; not ruled) | **Every consent signed on `/e-consent` is written with `applicationId = null`, so no application-scoped gate can see it — the borrower's Loan Estimate stays locked behind a consent the product already told them is complete.** Walked live on a fresh `active_buyer` file: `/e-consent` displayed **"Pending 0 · Completed 6 · Total Required 6"** while, at the same moment, `GET /api/loan-applications/225596f6-…/loan-estimate` returned **403 `{"code":"CONSENT_REQUIRED","consentType":"e_disclosure"}`** and `/api/consents/me` showed `{consentType:"e_disclosure", consentGiven:true, applicationId:null}`. Cause: `EConsent.tsx:118-124` posts only `{consentType, templateId, templateVersion}`; `applicationId` is **optional** in the POST schema (`consents.ts:75`) so the row lands null, and the gate reads `eq(borrowerConsents.applicationId, applicationId)`. **Escape hatch makes it worse, not better:** signing the same disclosure again from the LE page's gate card writes a **second** `e_disclosure` row *with* `applicationId` set (403 → 409), leaving **two rows for one disclosure, one orphaned**, on a record type carrying a tamper-evidence content hash. ⚠️ **Not a blanket fix** — `consentGate.ts:38-49` deliberately supports null-application rows via `hasUserConsent` for pre-application consents (e.g. `tax_document_use` in the renter incubator), so the fix is a per-type scope decision. DESIGN_SYSTEM §13 Agreement; the repo's silent-success class | `client/src/pages/borrower/EConsent.tsx:118-124`; `server/routes/borrower/consents.ts:71-76`; `server/storage/locksAndConsents.ts:152-157`; `server/consentGate.ts:31-34,38-49,177-185`; `server/routes/underwriting/delivery.ts:81` | open — walked 2026-08-20 on `main` @ `c23079b5` ([journey-walk j2](../routines/reports/2026-08-20-journey-walk-j2.md)) |

Clicking through lands on `/e-consent`, which says nothing is pending — a loop with no exit. Cause: `dashboard.ts:306` hardcodes `requiredConsentTypes = ["credit_pull","disclosure","privacy_policy"]`, while the types the product actually writes are `fcra_disclosure, privacy_policy, e_disclosure, credit_authorization, anti_steering, tax_document_use`. **`credit_pull` is documented as never written** — `creditConsents.ts:21-23`: *"`credit_pull` and `monitoring` appear in the schema's taxonomy comment but no code path has ever written them"*. Bare `"disclosure"` is likewise never written, and neither matches `CONSENT_TYPES` (`compliance.ts:514-526`) — **three consent vocabularies are in play**. | F-051 | 8 | defect | **P0** | yes (Fannie ULDD; Selling Guide A2-2-04 / A3-4-03) | **The delivered MISMO package tells the wholesale lender the AUS said `Approve` — always.** `server/mismo.ts:862` emits `AutomatedUnderwritingRecommendationDescription` as the compile-time literal `"Approve"`, inside a block gated only on `isApprovedGradeLoanAppStatus` (`shared/loanApplicationStatus.ts:96-108`, a set that includes `conditional`). `application.ausRecommendation` (`approve_eligible|approve_ineligible|refer|refer_with_caution`) appears **nowhere** in `server/mismo.ts`. **Reproduced twice, independently, by probe against the exact function the lender path calls (`buildLenderPackage`):** input `ausRecommendation:"refer"` → emitted `Approve`. **Reachable today** — `brokerSubmissionReadiness.ts:166-172` makes a non-approve recommendation a *warning*, and `:242-243` admits stages in `"attention"`, so the file reaches `submitToWholesaleLender` and the package persists to the immutable hashed `lender_submissions.mismoPackageXml`. `approve_ineligible` is trivially reachable (LTV>97, `ausSubmission.ts:177-180`). **The decisive evidence is 11 lines above the bug:** `:843-850` refuses to claim `DesktopUnderwriter` precisely because a delivery file must never assert an AUS artifact it does not have — the code states the rule and breaks it three lines later. compliance-auditor verdict **CONFIRMED-VIOLATION** against Selling Guide A2-2-04 ("All data pertaining to the mortgage loan is complete, accurate, and not fraudulent") and A3-4-03 (fraud varieties; notification duty attaches on *reasonable basis*, **no intent element**). Broker channel relocates but does not reduce exposure: the misstatement runs Homiquity→wholesale lender, contaminating the lender's own A2-2-04 warranty. **Explicitly NOT ECOA and NOT FCRA** — §1002.9(g) fetched verbatim; the error runs in the *favorable* direction so no adverse-action duty attaches, and an AUS recommendation is not a consumer report. Using those regimes would weaken the finding | `server/mismo.ts:862` (block `:841-875`); `shared/loanApplicationStatus.ts:96-108`; `server/services/brokerSubmissionReadiness.ts:166-172,242-243`; `server/services/ausSubmission.ts:177-180` | **FIXED** 2026-08-18 — PR [#545](https://github.com/barakatammre84/Homiquity/pull/545), merge `1eb8fdb`, **re-verified on `main` this pass, not taken from the PR**: `server/mismo.ts:894` now reads `application.ausRecommendation` through `mapAusRecommendation` (`:194-202`), and `:895` gates the whole `AUTOMATED_UNDERWRITINGS` container on a non-null result — so an absent or unrecognised recommendation **omits the container** rather than asserting one. That is the register's own prescribed direction, adopted verbatim, and the fix's docstring (`:176-193`) cites F-051 and the `:405-408` / `:846-850` precedents it follows. `AutomatedUnderwritingCaseIdentifier` is deliberately still not emitted (`:888-891`) so the simulator's `sim-du-<sha1>` cannot reintroduce the bug in another element — which is why **F-068 stays open**. The compile-time literal `"Approve"` no longer exists in the file. **This was the program's only open P0; open P0 count is now 0.** |
| F-052 | 8 | defect | **P1** | yes (URLA / ULAD v1.8 §1d) | **No organic borrower with under 2 years at their current job can ever pass GSE submission readiness.** `server/services/mismoValidation.ts:183` recognises a prior job only where `employmentType === "previous" \|\| "prior"`; `:188` makes three prior-employer fields required once current tenure < 2 years; `:711-716` promotes each to a `criticalError`. **No product surface can write either value** — exhaustive writer search across `client/ server/ shared/ tests/ scripts/ migrations/` finds only `"current"` (`URLAForm.tsx:404`, `server/routes/borrower/urla.ts:530`, `seedDemoFile.ts:90`) and `"additional"` (`urla/EmploymentSection.tsx:46`); the UI has no "previous employer" concept at all (`grep -i 'previous\|prior\|former'` → 0 hits). The column is `varchar(50)` with **no enum and no CHECK**. Verified by A/B/C probe: current job >2yr ⇒ 0 critical errors; <2yr + a fully-filled second job typed `additional` (the only thing the UI can write) ⇒ 3 critical errors, gate **blocked**; same file typed `previous` ⇒ clean. **The codebase already knows this discriminator is wrong** — `server/mismo.ts:579-583` fixed the identical bug in the emitter and left the validator. Client: `KycReviewQueue` mounted at the top of the staff Compliance tab. Four safety properties, each mutation-tested: (1) `"cleared"` is never a server-written literal — only the Zod-validated `decision`; (2) a settled screening returns **409**, never a silent re-decision; (3) adverse sub-checks are **preserved** — only `pending_review` advances, so a `flagged` OFAC hit survives an overall clearance; (4) clearance expires after `KYC_CLEARANCE_VALIDITY_DAYS` (365), since line 315 skips re-screening only while unexpired. Also fixed the `entries.length === 0` short-circuit: wiping a scope entirely used to be the *cleanest* way to pass. `verifyHashChain` now returns **`coverage`** (`headAnchored`, `sequenceCoverage`, `tailAnchored`, `tailAnchoredFromSequence`) so a caller cannot mistake "the entries I was handed link to each other" for "nothing was removed" — those were the same `{valid:true}` before, and that ambiguity *was* the finding. **No backfill** of tips, per CLAUDE.md: a synthesized tip would bless an already-truncated end as authoritative; pre-existing chains report `tailAnchored: false` until their next real append. **Landmine defused en route:** `scripts/reanchor-audit-chains.ts` rewrites every `entryHash`, so a pre-existing tip would name a hash that no longer exists and the tail check would fire a *false* truncation alarm the moment anyone re-anchored — the script now re-points existing tips (UPDATE, never INSERT, preserving the no-backfill rule). `tests/auditChainTruncation.test.ts` (13) + 4 in `mcpAudit` + 2 in `auditReanchor`; **8/8 mutations caught**. | F-008 | 9 | defect | P1* | yes (TCPA) | SMS webhook has no signature verification → forged START re-subscribes an opted-out number; *blocker only if SMS live at launch*. Scope was wider than the START leg the row names: unauthenticated, the endpoint was a lead-state mutation primitive — a forged **STOP** suppressed any phone number in the canonical ledger and flipped every matching lead to do-not-contact | `server/routes/webhooks.ts:21` | **FIXED** 2026-08-06 — `X-Twilio-Signature` verified against `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN` in `server/services/twilioSignature.ts` (HMAC-SHA1 over URL + byte-sorted params, base64, `timingSafeEqual`), transcribed from Twilio's published algorithm and pinned by **Twilio's own worked example** as a test vector — the hand-rolled implementation is only defensible while that vector passes. JSON bodies additionally verify `sha256(rawBody)` against the signed `bodySHA256` param; a JSON request without that param is rejected rather than treated as authenticated on a URL-only signature. Fail-CLOSED in production when the token is unset (503, the `PLAID_WEBHOOK_SECRET` posture); outside production an unset token keeps the provider-agnostic path usable, but a token that IS set is enforced in every environment. **The signing key is the account AUTH TOKEN, not an API key secret** — they are not interchangeable, hence its own env var. Opt-out fail-safe narrowed to what it can honestly mean post-auth: an *authentic* STOP still suppresses even if the lead sweep throws (ledger write first, sweep best-effort); honoring an *unverified* STOP was the vulnerability, not a safety property. Rejections go to `captureMessage` (without phone/body) because a dropped real opt-out is a TCPA problem. 32 tests in `tests/twilioWebhookSignature.test.ts`. **Residual, inherent to Twilio's scheme: no timestamp/nonce ⇒ replay is possible** — bounded by idempotent writes except for a replayed START re-subscribing a number that has since sent STOP; needs MessageSid de-duplication, tracked as **F-050** |
| F-050 | 9 | defect | P3 | yes (TCPA) | Inbound-SMS signature verification does not stop **replay**: Twilio's signature carries no timestamp or nonce, so a captured request can be re-sent. Writes are idempotent, so the only non-redundant case is replaying a captured **START** to re-subscribe a number that has since sent STOP. Needs `MessageSid` de-duplication (a stored seen-set with a retention window) | `server/routes/webhooks.ts`, `server/services/twilioSignature.ts` | open — carved out of F-008 at fix time; not a blocker until SMS is live |

The borrower-reachable link is at `client/src/pages/borrower/borrowerDashboard/LoanDetails.tsx:99-100`, pinned by `LoanDetails.test.tsx:62-82` (href, label, and both negative cases). **This unblocks the TRID chain the finding was really about:** `leIssuedDate` is stamped only when the borrower retrieves their own LE (`delivery.ts:95` — staff previews deliberately do not count) and that is the sole writer repo-wide, so the stamp had never fired from a UI click and `trid.ts:118`'s `leOverdue` made a TRID-triggered file permanently unadvanceable on the 4th business day. Original finding text retained below. — **CONFIRMED (core), two sub-claims REFUTED.** (1) "The borrower can never retrieve their LE" is **wrong** — the endpoint is `isAuthenticated`+`requireConsent` with no role gate, so direct API callers succeed; dev DB shows **4 of 176** applications with `le_issued_date`, all QA curl identities, never a UI click. (2) "Every file shows a false *TRID Deadline Passed*" is **wrong on both words** — with `tridTriggeredAt` null the predicate defaults `true` (`loanEstimate.ts:648`), and **173 of 176** rows are null; exactly **1 of 176** renders red, and where it renders it is **truthful**. ⚠️ **The inverted claim is the real finding: 173/176 files render an affirmative green "TRID Compliant" badge derived from a null due date.** Chain with F-079 (independent defects at one seam) |

| D-0818-01 | -- | doc-drift | **P1** | no | **`CLAUDE.md` froze an environment-dependent fact as a permanent premise, and it went stale: it tells every session that all four authoritative regulatory sources are blocked. All four return HTTP 200 with genuine section text.** ⚠️ **Correction to this finding as first written — the claim was TRUE when authored**, not wrong at origin: the ledger records `CONNECT 403` on **2026-08-04/05** in six entries' notes (`regz-1026-36d2-dual-compensation`, `trid-1026-19e3-fee-tolerance`, and others). The defect is that a *network observation* was written down as a *standing rule*, so it could never expire. The file states verbatim: *"every authoritative source (`ecfr.gov`, `consumerfinance.gov`, `govinfo.gov`, `law.cornell.edu`) is blocked from this environment, so the text cannot be fetched from inside a session."* **Probed directly this run** (`curl -s -m 15 -o /dev/null -L -w "%{http_code}"`): `ecfr.gov` API **200**, `consumerfinance.gov` **200**, `govinfo.gov` **200**, `law.cornell.edu` **200**. §1026.36(e)(3)(i)(C) was retrieved verbatim in-session (67 KB) from `https://www.ecfr.gov/api/renderer/v1/content/enhanced/current/title-12?chapter=X&part=1026&section=1026.36`. **The claim is load-bearing in two directions at once**: it is the stated reason ledger entries sit unverified, *and* the stated reason a Reg Z reading may never be asserted. **The "five" figure was accurate on 2026-08-06 and is now 11, spanning four regimes** — Reg Z, FCRA, Reg V, CROA, plus an Illinois fee schedule — so the stale premise is blocking more than Reg Z. **Verified by content, never by status code** (a 200 is not evidence): each body was grepped for the actual rule text, and the govinfo PDF's deflate streams were decompressed to confirm 78 occurrences of "loan originator" and the verbatim (C) clause. 12/12 stable across three rounds. Cost observed today: two separate agents in this very run declined to rule Reg Z questions citing the blockade — one after a `curl` that returned a **302 canonicalisation redirect** (adding `subpart=E`) which it read as failure; following the redirect yields **200 in 0.44 s**. Meanwhile the one source `CLAUDE.md` *does* instruct agents to fetch — the Fannie Loan Delivery job aid — is the only one genuinely **403** | `CLAUDE.md` (Regulation Z source-of-truth section); probe output in the 2026-08-18 qa-sweep report; `docs/reg-z/README.md` | **FIXED 2026-08-18** (founder-directed, outside this routine's §6 territory — deviation recorded in the report). `CLAUDE.md`'s Reg Z section now (a) states that **procurement, not network access**, is the reason entries sit unverified; (b) replaces the permanent claim with a **probe-first instruction plus a working recipe, verified to run verbatim**; (c) records both observed states with dates, so neither the old claim nor its opposite is asserted as permanent; (d) lists **four ways a reachable source looks blocked** — the eCFR 302, `WebFetch` truncation, the ~125-char quote ceiling, and **govinfo's soft 404** (its CFR *HTML granule* returns 200 with 44 KB of `<title>Page Not Found</title>` — found while writing the fix); and (e) preserves the rail verbatim. **The rail was NOT relaxed** — founder decision; strengthens U-26 and pairs with U-29. The *other* half of the premise is still true: `docs/reg-z/` holds no authoritative text. **This routine did NOT relax the rail on the strength of its own finding** — per routines/CHARTER §1b, a rail the machine can relax for itself is not a rail, so every Reg Z reading in today's rows stays flagged-not-asserted. Two sub-fixes: replace the blanket "blocked" sentence with the working eCFR API recipe **and** the ~125-character-per-quote ceiling the fetch tool enforces (ask subparagraph-by-subparagraph); and record that the job aid is 403, so `CLAUDE.md`'s compliance step #2 has **no executable path** |

No unique constraint on `(application_id, borrower_sequence_number)` (`compliance.ts:626-677`). Client-side (folded in from the UX pass): `HmdaDemographics.tsx:82-131` renders one form with **no sequence control and no borrower name** — while its sibling `urla/DemographicsSection.tsx:25` *does* label `"(Primary Borrower)"`/`"(Co-Borrower)"`, so the fix has a working pattern one directory over. | **Nondeterminism proven empirically, not argued from the missing ORDER BY** — same-shape temp table: `after insert, LIMIT 1 → seq=1 "primary"` / `after re-saving seq1, LIMIT 1 → seq=2 "coapp"`. The unordered `LIMIT 1` returns the primary while the heap is pristine and **flips to the co-applicant the moment the seq-1 row is updated**, which every URLA re-save does. Steady state, not a rare race. **The dashboard gate does not close it:** `dashboard.ts:181-182` sets `hmdaStatus` on ANY row and `LoanDetails.tsx:75-83` hides the link when complete — but that value is under `dashboardKeys.root()` (`queryClient.ts:259-261`) which the URLA save never invalidates (`URLAForm.tsx:402,427-429`), with `staleTime: 5*60*1000`, so the link stays live for five minutes exactly when two rows exist. `/hmda/:id` is a plain `BorrowerPage` (`App.tsx:449-451`), no gate. **Second harm:** `dashboard.ts:118-121` measures completeness by row presence, so **the co-borrower is never prompted at all.** Dated: route is original (`f89838cb`); `borrowerSequenceNumber` arrived next day (`f19f85b6`, co-borrower URLA intake) and the route was never revised — ~2.5 months live. | open — feature-reviewer + ux-reviewer (independently, from opposite ends) → finding-verifier CONFIRMED ×2. **Secondary note, API-only:** `borrowerSequenceNumber` is an accepted request field the lookup ignores, so a crafted POST can leave two rows at seq 2, after which `fairLendingAnalysis`'s `seq=1` join drops the file from monitoring entirely — the shipped page cannot do this (`hmdaMapping.ts:80-120` never sends it). |

Both denial seams and the manual route funnel through `generateAdverseAction`, so there is no second path — 100% of notices ever generated. **The "CFPB supervises us under 12 U.S.C. 5514" defence fails on the Appendix's own words:** *"This list is not intended to describe agencies' enforcement authority for ECOA and Regulation B."* — listing is decoupled from enforcement authority. **P2 not P1:** the notice still carries correct specific reasons, correct creditor identity and correct FCRA attribution, and the misdirection points at an agency that does take mortgage complaints — misleading, not a broken workflow. | open — compliance-auditor CONFIRMED → finding-verifier CONFIRMED, downgraded P1→P2. ⚠️ **Cites open counsel Ask 2** (`knowledge-base/compliance/LAUNCH_COUNSEL_PACKET.md:52-70`; `COMPLIANCE_COUNSEL_REVIEW.md:88-90` item 1; in-code `COUNSEL:` note at `creditCatalogs.ts:9-11`) — this row's new contribution is that **Appendix A mechanically settles the CFPB-vs-FTC half**. Recorded rather than quietly re-edited: this row has now been wrong in **both** directions in one day, which is itself the lesson — *reachability is a property of every path into a component, not of the one path you happened to probe.* |

The payload also carries `email`, `failedLoginAttempts` and `lockoutUntil` in the same object — **credential material paired with the account it opens** — plus, on the referrals route, **67 loan-application columns to an external partner** including `loCompensationBps`, `hmdaDenialReasons`, `preUwFlags` and `aiAnalysis`. | **Live, HTTP 200.** `GET /api/broker/referrals` as `lo` → 2,949 B, `passwordHash = '8eaffe374471b249f86edc48e561418590f38b2dc3af31f537ebef01cf03dc47…39ee8e3e0149c4752aca668641b29e9c'` (digest + salt), all 19 user columns. `GET /api/admin/stats` as admin → **`passwordHash` ×10, 3 with real non-null digests** (e.g. `'9ed33cc45aa3f8a7672c8fcaece9071c…'`). `GET /api/applications/:id/team` as the **application owner** (`buyer@test.com`) → 200 with the `passwordHash` key present on each staff member. `grep -rn "Join(users," server/` → 7 sites; 4 leak, 2 project correctly, 1 is a script. `grep -rln passwordHash tests/` → **0 files**. | open — **the program's only open P0.** feature-reviewer(UX lens) → finding-verifier **CONFIRMED, and the verifier found a leak the reviewer missed** (`/api/admin/stats`) plus a fourth latent site. ⚠️ **A dev-seed artifact nearly hid the worst leg:** all 8 staff accounts in the dev DB have `password_hash IS NULL` (**473 of 485 users overall DO have one**), so the borrower→staff-team leg returns the key with a `null` value here. The serialization is unconditional — **the null proves the field is shipped, not that it is safe**; a broker or realtor deal-team member (all of whom have hashes) is an intended configuration. **NOT a reopen of F-007** (closed 2026-07-12, `f5023c14`, correctly scoped to `/api/admin/users`, and **verified still working this run** — 230,339 B, zero hits): a correctly-closed row should not be misrepresented. **The real story is that the 2026-07-12 point-fix was never swept, and the repo has since written the same three-line strip a SECOND time** at `server/routes/agent-broker/referralsCoBrand.ts:96-97` — *"Never egress auth-sensitive columns from a full users row, even to an LO: passwordHash and lockout state have no business in an API response"* — **in the same directory as the leaking `profileBroker.ts`.** Two written invariants, four unfixed sites, zero tests. **Severity ruled, not deferred:** "scrypt, not plaintext" is a mitigating control, not a reason the material may cross the boundary; "the recipient already sees the file" fails because the marginal disclosure is credential + lockout state, which no recipient is authorized for; F-007's lower rating was admin-only. The escalator is the **recipient set** — `broker`/`lender` are third-party companies. ⛔ **One question deliberately NOT answered from memory: whether a salted scrypt digest is "a password … that would permit access to an online account" under Illinois PIPA (815 ILCS 530/5 — IL is the launch state) or "unencrypted customer information" under 16 CFR 314.4(j). That decides whether this is a NOTIFIABLE event; it does not change the engineering verdict.** §9 security review required on the fix. |

Consequences: `getTasksDueForEscalation` filters `lt(tasks.slaDueAt, now)` (`taskEngine.ts:642-657`) so NULL never matches; and `computeSlaStatus` returns **`green` when `!task.slaDueAt`** (`:158-159`), so a live `by-role/PROCESSOR` call returned **322 rows, 266 with a null clock, all reported `slaStatus: "green"`**. | Live A/B, same admin session, same application — **with the real dialog payload both routes return `slaDueAt: None`**; only adding a `taskTypeCode` the client never sends makes `/api/task-engine/tasks` produce `slaDueAt: '2026-08-21T10:15:05.530Z'` and class `S2`. DB: `1663/1723` null; by trigger source `MANUAL` **1395/1397** null; `max(created_at)` = today, so the manual path **is still producing SLA-less tasks**. Seed tables are populated (`task_type_sla_mapping` 22 rows, `sla_class_configs` 6). | open — feature-reviewer → finding-verifier **CONFIRMED at P1, with the root cause corrected** (the reviewer's A/B silently included a `taskTypeCode` the client never sends). **"Is NULL intended?" — pressed and answered NO:** there is no `ASSUMPTIONS.md` in this repo, no code comment says so, and the opposite is on record — archived roadmap item **CS1** (`knowledge-base/archive/roadmap/CTO_ROADMAP_2026-08-06.md:214`) describes `{slaDueAt: null, slaClass: "S3"}` as **the defect it was closing**, and `CHANGE_LEDGER.md:42` says *"a task could sail past its `slaDueAt` forever."* This is a regression against a closed roadmap item. Cross-ref **F-0820-52** (even when it fires, escalation notifies nobody) — the two compose into "no SLA control exists". |

Refutations hunted and failed: no second staff-facing route (`grep -rn "deal-rescue\|dealRescue" server/routes/ client/src` → one route file, one client page); no notification on create (both creators are bare inserts — no `sendNotificationEmail`, no `notifications` row); `?status=` does not widen it (the `reportedByUserId` condition is unconditional and ANDed). **Acceptance question B**: an SLA badge counting down against a queue nobody can see is a promise the product cannot keep. |
| F-0820-54 | 11 / 13 | defect | **P1** | no | **Any authenticated user — including an external `lender` partner and the least-privileged `aspiring_owner` — can write an arbitrary free-text string into the borrower-journey provenance table, bypassing the entire transition matrix.** `server/routes/intelligence.ts:145-152` is `isAuthenticated` only — **no Zod, no `parseBodyOr400`, no `requireRole`, no whitelist on `trigger`** — and casts `toState as BorrowerState`. `server/services/borrowerStateMachine.ts:56-57` is `if (trigger === "manual_override") return true`, the one trigger that short-circuits `VALID_TRANSITIONS` wholesale. The column is `varchar("to_state",{length:50}).notNull()` (`shared/schema/intelligence.ts:308-309`) with **no CHECK and no enum** (verified against `pg_indexes`). | Reproduced as **`lender`**, an external partner role, on a clean account: `POST {"toState":"FV3_ARBITRARY_NOT_A_STATE","trigger":"manual_override"}` → **HTTP 200** `{"success":true,"fromState":"lead","toState":"FV3_ARBITRARY_NOT_A_STATE"}`, persisted to `borrower_state_history`. Not merely an off-enum member of a sibling vocabulary — **a free-text string of the caller's choosing**. Read poison confirmed: `getStateMetadata` is `return metadata[state]` over a `Record<BorrowerState,…>` (`borrowerStateMachine.ts:135-161`), so `GET /api/intelligence/state` afterwards returns `{"currentState":"FV3_ARBITRARY_NOT_A_STATE","availableTransitions":[]}` with the **`metadata` key absent** — any consumer reading `metadata.progressPercent` throws. | open — **CONFIRMED at P1, materially stronger than filed.** **The "it's dead surface, so it's harmless" escape hatch was tested and FAILS.** The route does have zero client callers (`grep -rn "state/transition" client/src` → 0) and sits in the Reality Map's "Borrower Intelligence" set — but `borrower_state_history` is **not a dead table**: `server/pipelineEngine.ts:703-704` and `server/services/loanAnalysis.ts:438-449` write through the same `transitionState` on live paths. A production path reads and writes what any authenticated caller can pre-poison, and **being uncalled by our own client is not a security control — an attacker does not need our client.** Downstream, restated precisely by the verifier: forging a **valid** state (e.g. `funded`) makes `optimizationEngine.ts:251-254` early-return `{synced:true}` so a genuine `updatePipelineStage` sync becomes a silent **no-op**; forging any of `withdrawn/denied/expired/funded/homeowner` suppresses re-engagement at `:389-391`. The write is self-scoped to `user.id`, so there is **no cross-tenant impact** — which is why P1, not P0. **§9 trigger: YES** (adding a role gate + Zod to an authenticated route). Audit the fix by **running `detectTriggers()`** on the diff, not by reading the trigger list. The repo invariant this breaks is written down in `CLAUDE.md`: *"never backfill a guessed value onto a provenance column — a NULL is an honest gap, a wrong value is a falsified record."* This route lets any authenticated user write a falsified record. |

Client impact: `AttentionRail.tsx:94` renders `Pipeline (67)` and `:106` keys rows on `applicationId` → duplicate React keys; `StaffDashboard.tsx:153` sums across the raw array → double-counted volume. | open — feature-reviewer + ux-reviewer independently (filed as `D11-f` and `UX11-j`; **one id, both proposals cited**) → finding-verifier **CONFIRMED at P2, explicitly declining PLAUSIBLE.** The proposer's own caveat — that the observed duplicate is `team_role='lo'`, a value **no server code writes** (`grep -rn "teamRole:" server/` → the only literal is `"loan_officer"`; DB: `loan_officer` 93, `lo` 2, `processor` 1) — is a claim about **today's data, not about the defect**. Two code facts make it reachable through the supported API: **no uniqueness anywhere** (`pg_indexes` on `deal_team_members` → pkey + three plain btree, none unique; schema declares only `index(...)`), and **no existence check on insert** (`server/routes/borrower/dealTeam.ts:65-77`, with `teamRole` a free-text `z.string().min(1)`). `assignLoanOfficer` is correctly idempotent but guards only `teamRole === "loan_officer"`. A staffer legitimately holding two roles on one file at a small shop reproduces it. |

| **"Assets and liabilities are also mis-scoped in the MISMO co-borrower export"** | workflow-verifier (2026-08-17) | **REFUTED** | Struck from F-080 before registration. **ASSET is not emitted at all** under `purpose: "loanDelivery"` (`server/mismo.ts:1180-1181`), and `docs/fannie-mae/uldd-implementation-guide.pdf` p.14 states Fannie **ignores** the ASSET container if provided. **LIABILITY is correctly a DEAL-level container** (`:1192-1195`), so pooling both borrowers' liabilities there is right, not a leak. Only the PARTY-omission and employment-misattribution halves are defects. (Whether per-owner liability attribution is *additionally* required is an Appendix-D question — U-22) |

Also **dark mode is fine** (9.627:1) and should be dropped from the finding. See ux-34 |

The guard is present and correct. The residual — that the page renders a borrower-only `ConsentGateCard` to a staff-only audience, so its sole recovery affordance can only produce a 403 toast — is registered as part of **ux-30**, not as an auth defect |
| public-funnel-04 (partial) | Domain 1 review (2026-08-05) | REFUTED | The `ApprovalStrength.tsx` missing-`SEOHead`/sitemap-entry half of this finding is false-premise: the route is deliberately `<Gated>` per `ARMED_LAUNCH_CHARTER_2026-07-07.md` §2 (approval-language surfaces barred pre-F1-license), consistent with the same documented exclusion policy that already covers `/rates/*` and `/apply` — not an oversight |

| **C-9-07 (no prohibited-basis denylist in the rule DSL)** | compliance-auditor (Domain 9) | **REFUTED as a defect → `roadmap` note only** | Proposed as *"`ruleEngine.ts:20-23` resolves an arbitrary dotted `field` path against a caller-supplied context and `underwriting-rules.ts:263-266` accepts `context: z.record(z.string(), z.any())`, so an authored rule could condition on race or marital status."* **REFUTED on three independent grounds.** (1) **Reality-Map false-positive class:** `grep -rn "executeRules"` → exactly one production caller (`underwriting-rules.ts:274`); `grep -rn "underwriting-rules" client/src` → **zero**; `grep -rn "services/ruleEngine" server` → one import, that same route. Underwriting Rules is one of the four named server-only unshipped subsystems, and the live path is `decisionEngine.ts → underwritingEngine.ts`, which never touches `ruleEngine.ts`. (2) **The exploit path does not exist as described:** the route is `requireRole("admin")` and the context is entirely caller-supplied — nothing threads `hmda_demographics` or any protected attribute into it; an admin would have to type the applicant's race into the request body themselves. (3) **The framing is wrong even if it were wired:** "an admin could author a discriminatory rule" is an inherent property of any authoring DSL, and a field-name denylist is trivially routed around by renaming the context key; the real control is Fair Lending governance over rule authoring plus the audited `RULE_EXECUTE` entry that already exists at `:284-287`. **Keep only as a roadmap line:** before wiring Underwriting Rules to any live decision path, add a prohibited-basis denylist and a Fair Lending review of the authoring surface. Do not register as an open defect — a P3 defect row against dead surface is the noise the Reality Map exists to suppress. |
| **WF7-F9-r1 (AVM confidence band discarded)** | workflow-verifier (Workflow 7) | **REFUTED (leg 1 of a split; leg 2 registered as F-0819-16)** | Proposed as *"`lifecycleEngine.ts:421-430` consumes only `avm.estimatedValue`, discarding the `confidence`, `valueLow` and `valueHigh` the adapter returns, so the PMI alert fires on a point estimate whose own low bound is 4 points the wrong side of the threshold."* **REFUTED on two grounds.** (1) `server/mcp/vendors.ts:170-175` **throws** if `HOUSECANARY_API_KEY` is set — an explicit, enforced deterministic simulation, and per the Charter determinism in a simulated vendor is not a defect. (2) **Decisively: the band carries zero information.** `:179-181` derives `confidence = 0.72 + seeded(seed + "conf") * 0.23` and `spread = estimatedValue * (1 - confidence) * 0.5` — both are hash outputs of the same address seed, not a measurement. Consuming them would propagate simulated noise into a borrower-facing figure, which is *worse* than discarding them. Whether a real AVM contract would supply a usable band is unknowable today and is a design question for when a contract lands. The PMI notification is also appropriately hedged (`:491`: *"Your **estimated** loan-to-value just reached X% — at or below the 80% threshold where PMI **can often** be removed"*) — no entitlement asserted. **The durable half — `equity_snapshots` has no provenance column — is registered as F-0819-16 (`gap`, P3).** |
| **WF-ux6-r (adverse-action notice buries the path forward)** | ux-reviewer (Domain 9) | **REFUTED** | Proposed as *"the notice opens with statute names and buries the recovery card 3.3 screens down; reorder it."* **REFUTED — the ordering is a documented, adjudicated constraint pinned by a test, not an oversight.** `client/src/pages/borrower/adverseActionRecoveryCopy.ts:1-8`: *"no denial vocabulary — the notice itself owns the decision disclosure, … subordinate to the notice: rendered after it, hidden from print. (2026-08-04 renter-incubation adjudication §4 Leg B, verified by `adverseActionRecoveryCopy.test.ts` against `shared/compliance/loCommsLint`)"*, repeated at the render site (`AdverseActionNotice.tsx:167-168`). The recommendation would reverse a deliberate decision. **The measurement was also unrepresentative:** the injected fixture's `noticeText` is **2,235 chars** while all four real records in the dev DB are **1,257–1,274** (the real one read is the no-consumer-report variant, which correctly omits the FCRA block per `creditAdverseActions.ts:293-334`) — so `screensToRecovery: 3.3` is inflated by a fixture 78% longer than anything the generator has produced. The "redundant reason/score/CRA" leg also fails: `:117-161` is the structured, scannable rendering of a `whitespace-pre-wrap` blob — the value the page adds, not duplication. |
| **WF-ux9-r (three staff surfaces contradict each other on borrower notification)** | ux-reviewer (Domain 9) | **REFUTED as framed; residual merged into `ux-24`** | Proposed as a §13.3 AGREEMENT failure across `SubmissionLifecycleControl.tsx:220-222` (*"The borrower is not notified of the denial automatically"*), `CreditTab.tsx:379-383` (*"If the borrower hasn't seen the in-app notice…"*) and `statusDecisions.ts:274-282` + `emailService.ts:347-351` (a `status_update` notification and an email). **REFUTED: the three strings describe three different events.** `SubmissionLifecycleControl` governs `PATCH /api/lender-submissions/:submissionId` — a **wholesale-lender submission** status (header comment at `:24-41`); `grep -c createNotification server/services/lenderSubmission.ts` = **0** and no notification in `submissions.ts:268+`, so its claim is **true of its own action**. `statusDecisions.ts` is the **application status** route — a different dialog, a different entity. `CreditTab`'s line is a conditional, not an assertion. The genuine residual — that the borrower can essentially never see the in-app notice, which is what makes CreditTab's "if" misleading — is **ux-24's** territory and is recorded there as a copy sub-item. |
| **WF-ux16-r (hand-rolled zero-states)** | ux-reviewer (Domain 9) | **REFUTED (leg 1 of a split; the glyph leg is `ux-0819-10`)** | Proposed as *"`AdverseActionNotice.tsx:87-96`, `ComplianceTab.tsx:135-139` and `CreditTab.tsx:330-338` hand-roll centred-icon zero-states, failing §8's 'the only sanctioned zero-state'."* **REFUTED as a documented deliberate cut.** §8 does name `ui/empty-state.tsx` as the sanctioned component (11 adopters), but the **same section** says: *"Roughly 18 surfaces still hand-roll the … look that reads as stock. **Replace them as their pages are converted.**"* — a documented backlog with a stated remediation policy, which charter rule 6 puts out of scope. Re-filing three of the eighteen adds nothing. **The glyph misuse is not covered by that backlog and is registered as `ux-0819-10`.** |

What is true — that the notification carrying it dead-ends on `/dashboard` — is **F-0819-07**. Load-bearing for whoever fixes it: `underwriting/compliance.ts:96-99` returns **only aggregate counts, no ids**, so the ComplianceTab card cannot link to a list even if someone wanted it to. |

The truth is worse and is registered as **F-0819-17**: that assertion pins the **buggy** behaviour green — its instant is one at which a Guam recipient receives a 5 AM solicitation. A finding that says "untested" invites someone to add a test; the real instruction is "change the existing assertion". |
| **d9-02-r ("the breach notification is the sweep's only durable artifact")** | feature-reviewer (Domain 9) | **REFUTED (premise); the surviving finding is `F-0819-07` at P2** | Proposed as part of a P1 that *"no client surface reads the sweep result, so a missed notification means the breach is invisible."* **REFUTED:** `server/routes/underwriting/compliance.ts:71-99` reads `getUndeliveredAdverseActions()` **directly**, and `StaffDashboard.tsx:117` → `ComplianceTab.tsx:54-56,98-113` renders an always-on destructive-red *"N past 30-day ECOA window"* KPI, pinned by `ComplianceTab.test.tsx:33-39`. An independent staff surface shows the breach count regardless of the sweep or the notification, so the alarm is a redundant nudge rather than the load-bearing control — which is why F-0819-07 is P2, not P1. |
| **CA10-b (the Reg B firewall is "guarded by nothing")** | compliance-auditor (Domain 10, 2026-08-20) | **REFUTED as framed — the real sub-case is now F-0820-22** | Proposed that `tests/complianceInvariants.test.ts`'s `AI_IMPORT_PATTERNS` targets only AI libraries, so *"the Reg B firewall is true by construction and guarded by nothing."* **The headline is false and was disproven by execution, not argument.** Control pair run on a throwaway edit to `server/services/decisionEngine.ts` (reverted; `git status --porcelain` empty): appending `import Anthropic from "@anthropic-ai/sdk"` → **`× server/services/decisionEngine.ts imports no AI service` — 1 failed / 78 passed, RED.** The AI firewall is guarded and the guard fires. `MODEL_RISK_GOVERNANCE.md:21`'s claim (*"CI fails the build if any decision-path module imports an AI service"*) is **literally true**. What is unguarded is narrower: a **non-AI heuristic scorer** — appending `import { generatePrediction } from "./predictiveEngine"` to the same file leaves the suite **green (79 passed)**. But that is a *latent* guard gap with **no live defect** (`predictiveEngine` has exactly two importers today, `routes/data-intelligence.ts:30` and `services/riskBrief.ts:6`, neither in the decision path), and it is a sub-case of **F-014** ("`complianceInvariants.test.ts` is 100% source-grep — passes on wrong logic"). **Merged into F-014, which stays P1**, carrying the two executed control runs. Two real absentees recorded there: `server/services/pricingAdapter.ts` and `server/services/creditAdverseActions.ts` are decision-path modules missing from `DECISION_PATH_MODULES`. |
| **CA10-g (Reg B marital terminology "enforced only by a React dropdown")** | compliance-auditor (Domain 10, 2026-08-20) | **REFUTED as framed → the defensible core is F-0820-39 (P3)** | Three of the four supporting legs collapse. (1) **There is no React dropdown for that column** — `grep -rn "intelligence/profile" client/src` → **zero client callers**; nothing in the product writes `borrower_profiles.maritalStatus`, so the constraint is enforced by *nothing* and there is no UI to be a "last line of defence". (2) **The live marital-status inquiry is a different table and is CORRECT** — `client/src/pages/borrower/urla/PersonalInfoSection.tsx:144-148` renders exactly `Married`/`Separated`/`Unmarried (Single, Divorced, Widowed)` into `shared/schema/lendingUrla.ts:53`; that is the surface an examiner looks at. (3) **The claimed "fail-open decisioning input" is on a documented dead surface** — re-verified rather than deferred to the Reality Map: `grep -rn "intelligence/lender-matches" client/src` → zero client callers, routes are `requireRole(...INTERNAL_STAFF_ROLES)`, so an unvalidated `citizenshipStatus` cannot reach a live gate. (4) The `matchAndPriceBorrower` supporting grep was a **misread of a same-named function in a different file** (`optimizationEngine.ts:455`). Net: a latent trap on an unwired subsystem, **P3**, recorded as F-0820-39. |
| **D10-d ("Automation Rate is structurally pinned at 100%")** | feature-reviewer (Domain 10, 2026-08-20) | **REFUTED as framed → residual is F-0820-41 (P3)** | The load-bearing premise — *"`emitBorrowerAction` is the only emitter that would write `automationTriggered:false`"* — is disproven twice. `server/services/documentConfidence.ts:161` (`markHumanReviewCompleted`) and `:194` (`recordHumanReview`) both call `emitEvent(...)` passing **no** `automationTriggered`, which `analyticsEventPipeline.ts:44` defaults to `false`; and `markHumanReviewCompleted` is **client-reachable** at `server/routes/documents.ts:549` on every staff verify/reject. The DB confirms it has fired: `select … where automation_triggered = false` returns a genuine `document / human_review_completed` row dated **2026-07-17**, predating this review programme. Over all time the rate is 521/523; over the metric's real 30-day window, 164/165 = 99.39%. The metric is **misleading** (5 of 7 emitters are never called, so it measures a self-selected sample) but it is **not a tautology**. |
| **D10-e ("the client route gate is wider than the server gate")** | feature-reviewer (Domain 10, 2026-08-20) | **REFUTED as framed → merged into F-0820-25** | The drift is real but **the route gate is the correct side.** `client/src/lib/routeGates.ts:36-37` states the intent outright — *"Staff surfaces, including the external broker/lender partner roles"* — and `/staff-dashboard` is the shared staff dashboard whose other tabs a broker legitimately uses; narrowing `ROUTE_GATES.staff` would evict brokers from the whole product. **F-0818-13**'s precedent and `tests/routeGateDrift.test.ts:190-207` (*"fixed by NARROWING the client, never by widening the server"*) apply directly. The actual defect is the one line the finding did not cite: the unconditional `<TabsTrigger value="intelligence">` at `client/src/pages/staff/StaffDashboard.tsx:385-388`. Separately confirmed: `routeGateDrift.test.ts` does **not** cover these endpoints (`grep -n "staff-dashboard\|Intelligence\|data-intelligence"` → zero hits) — but that gap is **already recorded inside F-0818-13's notes**, so it is not a new finding either. |
| **D10-r ("the predictive model version is never surfaced")** | feature-reviewer (Domain 10, 2026-08-20) | **DOWNGRADED to an observation — no requirement is violated** | Facts hold (`predictiveEngine.ts:236` writes `modelVersion:"v1"`; `select model_version, count(*) from predictive_snapshots` → `v1 / 44`; the return type at `:46-57` omits it and no client renders it), but nothing requires otherwise, and it was looked for. `MODEL_RISK_GOVERNANCE.md:50-51` (MR-3/MR-4) requires model lineage to be **persisted** — it is, in the column that exists for it — and nothing in MRG requires a version in an API response. `:17` (M-5) classes the predictive engine as *"**Non-decisioning** (education/UX only)"*, outside MR-4's reproducibility obligations on decision snapshots. A single distinct value across 44 rows is what a v1 that has never been revised looks like. Not registered as a defect. |
| **"the funnel reports `funded: 0` while a funded application exists"** | workflow-verifier (analytics loop, 2026-08-20) | **REFUTED — backlog, not a wiring gap** | Filed as evidence that the `loanOutcomes` writers miss terminal transitions. Census refutes it: **every** terminal application lacking a `loan_outcomes` row has `updated_at ≤ 2026-07-12` — the date of `f5023c14` (#136), the commit that wired the writers — and every terminal transition after it has a row. The one funded application dates to 2026-07-03. **F-002 stays closed.** |
| **"`expired` has no `recordStageTimestamp` case, so the funnel denominator is unresolvable"** | workflow-verifier (analytics loop, 2026-08-20) | **REFUTED — latent, and nothing writes the state** | `expired` is a real member of `LOAN_APP_TERMINAL_STATUSES` and the switch has no case for it, but **nothing writes `loan_applications.status = "expired"`**: `letterExpiry.ts:30,63` updates `preApprovalLetters`/`preQualificationLetters`, and the only other `status:"expired"` writers are KBA sessions and application invites. Zero applications are in that state. Worth a one-line comment in the switch; not a finding. |
| **FV-e — `server/mismo.ts:373-375` asserts "Unmarried" for an unmapped marital value** | finding-verifier (2026-08-20) | **MERGED into F-055, not refuted** | `text: maritalMap[personalInfo.maritalStatus?.toLowerCase()] \|\| "Unmarried"` substitutes a **positive** marital-status value for an unrecognised one in the GSE export — precisely the pattern F-055 catalogues (`mapPropertyUsage(undefined) → "PrimaryResidence"`, `mapLoanPurpose(null) → "Purchase"`, etc.), on a column (`shared/schema/lendingUrla.ts:53`) that is a bare `varchar(50)` with no DB-level enum. Constrained today by the correct 3-option dropdown, so it can only fire on a non-UI write. Added to F-055's list rather than opening a row (D-012 discipline). |

## Escalations — need founder/source confirmation before any fix (compliance-first)

| **U-18** | **Is a staff-set `withdrawn` or `suspended` an adverse action under 12 CFR 1002.2(c)?** The rule text and its five (c)(2) exceptions were fetched verbatim and **none covers a creditor-initiated closure of a pending application**; Supplement I comment 9-2's carve-out is keyed on an application the applicant **"expressly withdraws"**, which is exactly the fact `loan_applications` records nowhere. Two sub-questions are legal calls: does `suspended` (a creditor-side hold of indefinite duration, and *reversible*) start the 1002.9 clock, and must the platform capture a withdrawal **initiator** to evidence the exemption. **The engineering half is settled and fixable now** -- see F-062. Decides whether F-062 is P2 or P1 | see linked findings above |

Still blocked: `ecfr.gov` HTML (302 to `unblock.federalregister.gov`) and **`singlefamily.fanniemae.com` (403)**, which is why the Loan Delivery job aid remains unusable. **Consequences:** F-024 is now verifiable (and verified); U-8/HMDA and the U-11 FCRA/ESIGN cluster are likely resolvable against `/1003/` and `/1022/` without founder action; and `docs/reg-z/`'s shopping list is now fillable. **Lifting CLAUDE.md's "Reg Z readings are flagged, never asserted" rule is the founder's call, not a routine's** -- this run reported the changed premise and did not act on it | see linked findings above |

| U-11 | **FCRA and ESIGN primary sources are absent from the repo** — the third instance of one structural gap. The F-034/035/040 cluster (consent scope, disclosure fidelity, expiry, e-signature evidence) cannot be adjudicated against federal authority: `docs/` holds only `fannie-mae`, `irs-forms`, `lender-programs`, `nmls`, `nmls-safe`; the regulatory ledger has **one** FCRA entry (a version pointer whose `codeRef` is stale) and **zero** ESIGN entries — so the platform's entire ESIGN posture (`e_disclosure` gate, LE electronic delivery, `signature_type: "electronic"` on every credit consent) rests on no ledgered citation. Resolve by adding `docs/federal/` with (1) FCRA §604 / 15 U.S.C. §1681b, (2) E-SIGN 15 U.S.C. §7001 (consumer-consent + accurate-reproduction/retention), optionally (3) 12 CFR part 1022. **Pin exact subsections only once the documents land.** ⚠ **The meta-point, bigger than any one escalation:** `docs/` contains **zero consumer-protection statutory text**, yet L2 names four consumer-protection invariants — I5 (TRID/Reg Z), I6 (FCRA/ECOA), I7 (TCPA), I8 (ECOA/HMDA) — every one citing an authority with no local copy. **U-8, U-10 and U-11 are not three coincidences; they are three samples of the same gap**, and three consecutive compliance-auditor runs have returned UNVERIFIABLE on it. Standing up `docs/federal/` closes the class rather than the instances | `docs/`, `data/regulatory/regulatory-ledger.json`, `knowledge-base/L2_COMPLIANCE_AND_LOGIC.md` |
| U-12 | **The `consentType` → `pullType` coverage map is a legal reading, not an engineering choice.** Building the comparison closes F-035; deciding *which* consent covers *which* pull (does `credit_authorization` imply hard? does a soft consent permit a soft *tri-merge*?) encodes an interpretation of FCRA's written-instruction requirement and is UNVERIFIABLE locally (U-11). Implement the mechanism now with the strictest defensible mapping — **exact-match, deny by default** — and escalate the map. Same for F-040's expiry *value* (120 days) and whether it binds a funnel consent whose displayed text never mentioned any period. Also escalate: whether showing the full `FCRA_DISCLOSURE_TEXT` in the funnel is wanted — compliance-auditor advises **against** it unilaterally, since it would expand what the borrower authorizes (soft → soft+hard, 120-day, identity attestations) under a badge that says "soft credit check only" three times, which is UDAP-adjacent. Preferred posture needing no legal call: store the text actually rendered, keep the funnel genuinely soft-scoped, and let the gate enforce soft-consent→soft-pull | `server/services/creditPulls.ts`, `creditConsents.ts`, `client/src/pages/lending/PreApproval.tsx` |
| U-13 | **Should self-declared veteran status gate VA pricing at all?** (F-048) VA pricing is materially more favourable — **zero mortgage insurance** plus a **62.5bp** lower base rate (6.250 vs 6.875) — and today it turns on `isVeteran` or `preferredLoanType === "va"`, both borrower-writable and neither verified. That produces a priced, lettered pre-approval on an unverified claim, upstream of the URLA section-6 gate that would otherwise block delivery. **The engineering fix is easy and the policy call is not**, which is why this is escalated rather than patched: options run from (a) require a COE / entitlement artefact before VA pricing applies, to (b) price conventional until verification and re-price on proof, to (c) accept self-declaration for the *estimate* but label it conditional on COE. Each has a different UDAAP posture — (c) in particular depends on whether the pre-approval letter's conditionality is stated prominently enough to be non-misleading under Reg N §1014. Related: the letter surface prints `isVeteran ? "VA" : "CONV"` with no verification marker | `server/services/loanEstimate.ts:278,281,317`, `server/routes/lending/letters.ts:169,256`, `server/routes/profile.ts:33` |
